KONNECTOME - CHAT LOG 01

A VERBATIM RECORD OF ONE WORKING SESSION. THIS IS NOT A SOURCE DOCUMENT AND MUST NOT BE CITED AS ONE.

Session date: 2026-08-12. Session identifier 1e05a5d4-2103-4f46-9419-f89eb73a0b7d.

Written to this directory on 2026-08-12 by the owner's explicit request, transcribed from the session record on disk rather than reconstructed from anyone's memory of it.


WHAT THIS FILE CONTAINS, AND IN WHAT PROPORTIONS

Three kinds of entry, each labelled on its own line.

OWNER - the owner's messages, as typed.

ASSISTANT - the assistant's replies, as sent. Tool calls appear as single bracketed lines naming the tool, so a reader can see where work happened between turns without the log carrying several megabytes of command output.

BACKGROUND - the completion reports of delegated research agents. These are NOT a side of the conversation and are marked separately for that reason, but they are included because they carry the literature research this session paid for, and because a record that omitted them would have thrown away the most reusable thing in it.

WHAT IS NOT HERE: tool results, the assistant's internal reasoning, and the separate full transcripts of the delegated agents, which live outside this repository under the session's task directory.

A CORRECTION MADE WHILE WRITING THIS FILE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME CLASS OF ERROR THIS SESSION SPENT ITSELF GUARDING AGAINST. The first version of this header claimed the research appeared inside the owner-side messages. It did not - the research arrived as background notifications, which the first pass had filtered out, so the header asserted something the file did not contain. It was caught by grepping the finished file for names that had to be in it, Hallgren and Shannon, and finding zero. The claim is now true because the content was added, not because the sentence was softened.


WHY IT SITS IN THIS DIRECTORY, AND THE RULE IT SITS AWKWARDLY AGAINST

This directory's own README states the test for what belongs here: could this document be read by a neuroscientist who has never heard of the project that commissioned it? A chat log fails that test plainly, and by the letter of the rule it belongs among the build's own documents rather than in the corpus.

It is here because the owner asked for it here, and that is a sufficient reason. The tension is recorded rather than glossed, and this header exists so that no later session can mistake the file for source. It is a RECORD OF A CONVERSATION ABOUT the corpus, not a statement ABOUT THE BIOLOGY, and nothing in it carries any authority over any file beside it.

IT DOES CLOSE ONE REAL GAP. The repair hand-off in this directory records that a large amount of literature research was completed in this session, was never written into a chapter, and existed only in the session transcript rather than on disk. That is no longer true. A session picking up the unfinished work can now read what was already established, and in several cases what was already found to be wrong, instead of paying for those searches again.

HOUSE STYLE DOES NOT APPLY TO THIS FILE, deliberately rather than carelessly. Every other document in this directory forbids markdown characters and em-dashes. A verbatim record edited to satisfy a style rule would no longer be verbatim, which would defeat the only purpose it has.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[ASSISTANT - 2026-08-12 08:09:34]

Slice 74 is landed and merged to main.

WHAT THE HAND-OFF ASKED FOR

Caliper Filewright's queue item one: the blackboard's step rate and its wiring, together, per OBSERVATION-13.

That item is discharged.

THE PROBLEM

konnectome's working memory has been a complete, forty-five-test pack since slice 49 and reachable from no running loop ever since, because wiring it meant choosing how long a maintenance step takes and the corpus never says.

The obvious answer, one step per heartbeat, makes the mind forget in a sixth of a second while citing a chapter that says two to twenty seconds.

HOW THE NUMBER WAS ARRIVED AT

• The corpus was read again first, and returned a third statement of the lifetime that twenty-five slices had not counted.

• Derivation was tried before any value was chosen, and narrowed without settling: none of the three stated lifetimes divides into a whole number of ticks.

• A second refusal was reconsidered and declined, on the definition's own clause that a part unreachable from a live loop is not yet part of konnectome.

• Then a value was chosen and signed: one step per nominal second, on the ground that it is the only decade of konnectome's own tick whose implied lifetime lands inside the corpus's measured window. A test asserts that argument, not the number.

THE UNPLANNED FINDING

Four tests failed with the board empty at every tick, and the wiring was not at fault.

konnectome carries two live, declared, incompatible readings of its own heartbeat - one hundred ticks to the nominal second, and the scheduler's documented one tick per hour - a factor of three hundred and sixty thousand, invisible for dozens of slices because every construct in the loop moved once per tick and nothing ever multiplied.

The scheduler's defaults were deliberately not changed. That is a numbered decision, not an auto-fix, and it is OBSERVATION-23 and the next session's first queue item.

MEASURED STATE

• 1,132 tests across 41 packs, all green, up thirteen.

• Six gates at zero violations. Capstone story byte-identical against main, verified by running both.

• Method documents at version 64, tutorial at version 61 with sixty-three parts and current again, build log at seventy-three chapters.

Merged as [pull request 167](https://github.com/ai-university-aiu/konnectome/pull/167), commit 99d2132. New hand-off written, prior one swept in the same change.

One slice this session rather than two, which is now two sessions running - the hand-off names it and the reason rather than padding the count, and flags it for you to judge.

A plain-text copy is at [konnectome_slice_74_blackboard_step_rate_and_wiring.txt](/home/ccaitwo/output/konnectome_slice_74_blackboard_step_rate_and_wiring.txt).

Done and merged.

THE MOVE

docs/admin/ created; the ledger, build log and tutorial moved with git mv so their history follows them.

docs/ top level now holds exactly one file, and it is the current hand-off.

THE SWEEP, WHICH WAS THE LARGER HALF

• Five pointers in CONSTITUTION.md: the Fourth Commandment's ledger path, the Tenth's second copy of that same path, the Eighteenth's tutorial-uniqueness directory, the Nineteenth's build-log path, and the Twenty-Third's hand-off reading list.

• Three README links plus its directory tree, which described the old layout.

• One citation each in konnectome_0_definition_v2, design/06, design/08 and design/11.

• Four lines of the current hand-off, including its DOCS LAYOUT entry - that one is a reading list rather than a report, so leaving it would have sent the next session to three files that are not there.

WHAT I DELIBERATELY DID NOT REPOINT

Three lines inside the Commandments still name paths nothing lives at, and that is on purpose. "A new file; create it at build start", "Add the following to /docs/: konnectome_tutorial_v1.txt", and the Nineteenth's history of the build log's renames are records of dated acts, not pointers. Rewriting a record to agree with a later directory turns a true historical claim into a false one.

The same test excluded docs/archive/ (preservation language, not a reading list) and the forty-one superseded hand-offs (dated reports of past sessions - the hand-off protocol itself says to edit none of them). The cost is named rather than hidden: a session following a path out of any pre-today document will miss, and the ledger's reorganisation note is where it should land.

THE CONSTITUTION MOVED TO VERSION 28

Required, because five of its pointers had to follow. No Commandment added, removed or renumbered - still twenty-three - and no rule changed in any of the three. The version-history entry was written in the same change, as that file's own head requires.

I also wrote docs/admin/archive/ into the Eighteenth Commandment as a thing that must not be created, since the next session to bump the tutorial will be standing in a new directory with an obvious wrong move one keystroke away.

VERIFICATION

1,132 tests across 41 packs, all green. Six gates at zero violations. Capstone story unchanged, verified by running and comparing.

Recorded as a reorganisation note in the ledger and Chapter Seventy-Four in the build log. Merged as [pull request 168](https://github.com/ai-university-aiu/konnectome/pull/168), commit 6ac3ee5.

Plain-text copy at [konnectome_docs_admin_directory_move.txt](/home/ccaitwo/output/konnectome_docs_admin_directory_move.txt).

[ASSISTANT - 2026-08-12 08:26:30]

Done and merged.

THE ONE THING WORTH FLAGGING FIRST

This is not the same kind of act as this morning's docs/admin/ move. That one moved only pointers. This one CHANGES A RULE, because the current hand-off's location is written into the Twenty-Third Commandment's hand-off protocol.

The Constitution moved to version 29, with its history entry in the same change. No commandment added, removed or renumbered - still twenty-three.

WHAT DID NOT CHANGE

Exactly one hand-off stands outside docs/hand-off/ at any moment. It is always the one to read. The sweep is still a precondition. The naming convention is untouched. Superseded hand-offs still live in docs/hand-off/ , unarchived and still required reading.

THE MECHANICAL POINT

Step 2a sweeps OUT OF a directory and Step 2b writes INTO it. They are one mechanism wearing two step numbers. Repointing either alone leaves a protocol that still runs, still commits, still passes every gate, and quietly stops sweeping - which is the exact silent failure the version 13 amendment reordered those steps to prevent. Both were edited in one act.

WHY THIS IMPROVES ON VERSION 13 RATHER THAN REVERSING IT

Version 12 reversed this once and version 13 reversed it back, so I checked the reasoning rather than assuming.

All three of version 13's reasons survive. Its third and load-bearing one gets stronger: it wanted the latest hand-off to be FOUND rather than DERIVED, and its answer - "the only dated file in /docs/" - rested on a NEGATIVE, on what was not in that directory, which a reader must check rather than see. For fifteen versions that directory held four files and the rule needed a reader to know which three to ignore.

It now rests on a positive fact that announces itself, findable from a directory listing by a session that has read nothing at all.

I also wrote a prohibition into the Commandment: nothing else may go in docs/start-here/ . The thing that would put a second file there is not carelessness, it is helpfulness - a README, a quick-start, a checklist - and each destroys the property the directory exists for.

THE SWEEP

Three sites in CONSTITUTION.md, three passages in docs/hand-off/README.txt, README.md's tree, and the hand-off's own DOCS LAYOUT entry.

Version 13's own note was marked superseded in place rather than edited, since it is a record of a dated act.

A simplification fell out of it: the forwarding note for old paths had carried an exception since 2026-08-10, because the current hand-off's old-style path still resolved. That exception has lapsed - no old-style path resolves now, so the forwarding rule has no special case left.

VERIFICATION

1,132 tests across 41 packs, all green. Six gates at zero violations. Capstone story unchanged. The corpus-update-run trigger still counts 42 hand-offs recursively.

docs/ now holds no files at all - two moves in one day took it from four to none.

Merged as [pull request 169](https://github.com/ai-university-aiu/konnectome/pull/169), commit 6aa310d. Recorded as a second, deliberately separate reorganisation note in the ledger and Chapter Seventy-Five in the build log.

Plain-text copy at [konnectome_docs_start_here_directory_move.txt](/home/ccaitwo/output/konnectome_docs_start_here_directory_move.txt).

## 1. Document-shape pattern (Layers 4/5 through 11)

| Layer | OUTLINE | MANUSCRIPT | MODES_OUTLINE | MODES_MANUSCRIPT |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4+5 (merged) | yes (`LAYER_04_05_SLOW_VARIABLES_OUTLINE.txt`) | yes | yes | yes |
| 6 | yes | yes | yes | yes (+ 5th file, `LAYER_06_CHAPTER_TEMPLATE_v3.txt`) |
| 7 | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| 8 | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| 9 | **NO separate OUTLINE file** | yes | yes | yes |
| 10 | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| 11 | yes | yes | yes | yes |

- **Layer 9 has no standalone outline file.** Its outline is embedded as a chapter inside the manuscript: `LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT.txt:319` `CHAPTER 2. THE COMPLETE OUTLINE OF LAYER 9 CONSTRUCTS`. Likewise `THE_FIFTEEN_LAYERS_IN_BRIEF.TXT` is a verbatim extract of that manuscript's Chapter 1 (`:145`; the shared sentence appears at `THE_FIFTEEN_LAYERS_IN_BRIEF.TXT:89` and `LAYER_09_...MANUSCRIPT.txt:231`). `README.txt:50` acknowledges only "Two files, LAYER_09_MODES_ prefixed" — the absence is registered but not explained.
- **Layers 4/5 merge IS explained in the corpus's own text**, and the explanation is that these are not full catalogues at all: `LAYER_04_05_SLOW_VARIABLES_OUTLINE.txt:12` — "This is deliberately NOT a full catalogue of Layers 4 and 5 - those layers as wholes are substrate, and the framework abstracts them away." And `LAYER_04_05_SLOW_VARIABLES_MANUSCRIPT.txt:20` — "it is the dossier of their cognitive elite, in eighteen chapters and four parts". Actual count: 18 chapters, confirmed.

## 2. Layers with zero catalogue documents

**Layers 1, 2, and 3** (Subatomic, Atomic, Ionic/Simple Molecular) have no catalogue file of any kind.

The corpus is **effectively silent on the absence as an absence**. `README.txt` never mentions Layers 1-3 in its file inventory (lines 28-71 jump from the framework documents straight to "LAYERS 4 AND 5"). `TFELOAOTHNAES.txt` treats them only as boundary physics — `:267` "Layer 1 to Layer 2: nuclear and atomic physics... This boundary is essentially perfect" — which is an implicit justification (settled science, nothing to catalogue) but is never stated as a decision not to catalogue them. The nearest explicit reasoning is the Layer 4/5 "substrate... abstracts them away" line above, which is applied to 4/5, not to 1-3.

**Layers 12-15** also have zero *catalogue* documents; see next section.

## 3. Layers 12-15 are konnectome-facing, not neuroscience catalogues

All four self-identify in their own subtitle line and opening as design documents, not catalogues:

- `LAYER_12_FACULTY_SPECIFICATION.txt:3` "A design-facing document"; `:15` — "**not a parts catalogue but the SPECIFICATION** - the list of faculties the assembled machine must exhibit, against which every build is verified." Subtitle `:3` "the Acceptance-Test Catalogue for a Synthetic Mind."
- `LAYER_13_TUTOR_PROTOCOL.txt:15` — "Its engineering role... is METHOD rather than module... a synthetic mind inspired by the human one inherits that requirement as a **training-interaction design**."
- `LAYER_14_KNOWLEDGE_ENVIRONMENT.txt:15` — "Its engineering role... is the ENVIRONMENT... **a synthetic mind needs its own designed knowledge environment**."
- `LAYER_15_DESIGN_METHOD.txt:15` — "Its engineering role... is the DESIGN METHOD... translate directly into **the iteration discipline by which a synthetic mind is designed, tested, and improved**."

The README concedes exactly this and admits it breaks the directory's own subject rule: `README.txt:19` — "Layers 12 through 15 were written by the corpus author but **described the build rather than the biology**"; `README.txt:56` — "one design-facing document each. These four **turn from describing the biology** to stating what a system built from it would have to do, and they are the reason the subject test above is worded as it is."

**Plainly: the biology of Layers 12, 13, 14 and 15 — organism/behaviour, dyad/small group, community/society, evolution/species — is uncatalogued.** Those four layer numbers are occupied by engineering documents that only borrow the layer name. Combined with Layers 1-3, **seven of the fifteen layers have no catalogue of their biology**; only Layers 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11 are fully catalogued, with 4/5 partially and avowedly so.

## 4. Claimed counts vs actual — all six verify

| Claim (README) | Actual | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 85 non-neuronal cell types (L6) | 85 `^CHAPTER n` in `LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT.txt`; 85 `SYSTEMS INTERFACE:` blocks | match |
| 64 junction types (L7) | 64 chapters, 64 SUBSCRIBES, 64 PUBLISHES, 64 SYSTEMS INTERFACE | match |
| 71 canonical motifs (L8) | 71 chapters, 71 SYSTEMS INTERFACE | match |
| 44 systems (L10) | 44 chapters, 44/44 interface blocks | match |
| 26 operating states (L11) | 26 chapters, 26/26 interface blocks | match |
| five neuron catalogue volumes | 5 files; 60+63+62+56+60 = **301** chapters | match, and consistent with the 301-not-302 repair recorded at `README.txt:94` |

Two incidental discrepancies, both minor:
- `README.txt:8` and `:28` say "**forty-one** source documents" / "THE FORTY-ONE FILES"; the directory contains **42** files. The itemisation at `:40-70` sums to 42 (4+2+4+5+5+4+4+2+4+4+4+2 = 44 by heading, but the L9 pair and the framework pair overlap the guiding book). The headline number is stale relative to the cognition pair added the same day (`README.txt:66`).
- `LAYER_08_MICROCIRCUITS_MANUSCRIPT.txt` has 72 SUBSCRIBE/PUBLISH occurrences against 71 chapters; the extra pair is prose in the preface at `:32`, not a 72nd entry.
- `LAYER_06_MODES_MANUSCRIPT.txt` carries 386 `MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:` sections = 85 + 301 exactly, so the modes companion covers every cell chapter with no gap.

## 5. The seven Layer-Skipping Channels

- **Enumerated in exactly one place**: `TFELOAOTHNAES.txt:302` `PART FOUR. LAYER SKIPPING - HOW IT IS POSSIBLE, AND THE CATALOGUE OF EXCEPTIONS`, with the seven at `:318, 320, 322, 324, 326, 328, 330`. All seven are named: Steroid Hormone, Volume Transmission, Circumventricular and Neurohypophysial, Vagal and Interoceptive, Neuroimmune, Electromagnetic and Photic, Transgenerational Epigenetic. A definition and admission test precede them (`:308` the Re-encoding Test), plus two "minor" within-layer shortcuts at `:332` (olfactory bypass, hyperdirect pathway).
- **They do NOT receive the interface treatment.** Zero `SUBSCRIBE`/`PUBLISH` occurrences anywhere in `TFELOAOTHNAES.txt`. Each channel is a single prose bullet with source/target layers and a confidence tag ("very high", "high", "contested" at `:330`) — no SUBSCRIBES/PUBLISHES block, no mode register, no chapter. They are the only first-class construct class in the corpus with no interface specification.
- The document calls the collection "the Interface Exception Catalogue" (`:306`), but **no file by that name or content exists**; the phrase appears nowhere else in the corpus.
- Elsewhere the channels are only *referenced* in passing, and only 4 of the 7 by number: Channel 1 (`LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_OUTLINE.txt:259, :361`; `LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT.txt:5979, :9230`), Channel 3 (`OUTLINE:86`, `MANUSCRIPT:5979`), Channel 4 (`OUTLINE:430`), Channel 5 (`OUTLINE:132`). Channels 2, 6, 7 are never cited by number outside TFELOAOTHNAES.

## 6. Interface-block conformance to `LAYER_06_CHAPTER_TEMPLATE_v3.txt`

Three distinct structures exist; no single schema spans the corpus.

**Family A — full 20/21-aspect template (template-conformant):**
- `LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT.txt` — 85/85 on every sampled aspect header (WHAT IT IS, STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY, ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES, ENGLISH READABLE CODE, CODE SAMPLE, WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE, SYSTEMS INTERFACE).
- `neuronal_manuscript_vol1of5..vol5of5` — 56/60/62/60/63 SYSTEMS INTERFACE, matching chapter counts per volume, all aspects present.
- Note both carry **zero** literal SUBSCRIBES/PUBLISHES keywords; their interface is the prose `SYSTEMS INTERFACE:` aspect, exactly as the template specifies (`LAYER_06_CHAPTER_TEMPLATE_v3.txt:55`). Modes live externally, per `:7`.

**Family B — reduced aspect set plus explicit publish/subscribe bullets (partially conformant):** Layers 4/5, 7, 8, 10, 11. These have `ENGLISH READABLE CODE:`, `CODE SAMPLE:`, `WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:`, `SYSTEMS INTERFACE:` at full count, but **no** `WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:`, `STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:`, or `ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:` headers (0 in every one). They add machine-parsable bullets absent from the template: `• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).` / `• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).` at 18/64/71/44/26 respectively (e.g. `LAYER_08_MICROCIRCUITS_MANUSCRIPT.txt:230, :462, :686`). This is a *different, looser* structure than the template — reasonable given these are not cell types, but the README's claim at `:42` that the template is "the SCHEMA by which tooling can parse **every** chapter's interface blocks" is not sustained literally.

**Family C — non-conformant, its own structure:** `LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT.txt`. Zero `SYSTEMS INTERFACE:`, zero `ENGLISH READABLE CODE:`, zero `CODE SAMPLE:`. It uses a four-field regional schema of its own, declared at `THE_FIFTEEN_LAYERS_IN_BRIEF.TXT:131-147`: `INPUTS (SUBSCRIBE)` / `OUTPUTS (PUBLISH)` / `FUNCTION AND FACULTY` / `SYSTEM MEMBERSHIP:` (first instance `LAYER_09_...:353, 371, 387, 395`), across 122 regional entries. Keyword totals are asymmetric — 167 SUBSCRIBE vs 183 PUBLISH — because interfaces are also discussed in Chapter 3 (`:4025 CHAPTER 3. THE INTERFACES`), so the guiding book is the least tool-parsable of the catalogued layers despite being the corpus's declared source document (`README.txt:30`).

**Summary:** template-conformant = Layer 6 (both cell manuscripts). Loose variant = Layers 4/5, 7, 8, 10, 11. Non-conformant/own schema = Layer 9. The modes companions (all layers) share a fourth uniform structure — `MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:` + `THE MODE REGISTER` — at counts matching their parent catalogues exactly (386, 64, 71, 77, 44, 26), and are the most internally consistent part of the corpus.</result>

[ASSISTANT - 2026-08-12 08:39:29]

Every claim below was verified by grep or line-count against the corpus itself. Full write-up at [konnectome_neuroscience_library_gap_analysis.txt](/home/ccaitwo/output/konnectome_neuroscience_library_gap_analysis.txt).


THE HEADLINE, BEFORE THE COMPLAINTS

Most of what you would expect to be missing is not missing.

The peripheral, autonomic and enteric systems, the cerebellum, the basal ganglia, every sensory modality end to end, grid and place and time cells, language, consciousness theories beyond global workspace, the blood-brain barrier, glymphatic clearance, microglia, the gut-brain axis - all covered. The endocrine half of the title is honoured better than most textbooks honour it. Every count the corpus claims for itself checks out: 85 cell types, 64 junctions, 71 motifs, 44 systems, 26 states, 301 neuron chapters, 41 source documents.

So the real gaps are structural, and sharper than a first look suggests.


THE FILING GAPS

• SEVEN OF FIFTEEN LAYERS HAVE NO BIOLOGY CATALOGUE. Layers 1, 2 and 3 have no document at all. Layers 12 through 15 have one document each and every one of them self-identifies as design-facing, not as a catalogue. Only Layers 6 through 11 get the full four-file treatment.

• THE SEVEN LAYER-SKIPPING CHANNELS ARE THE ONLY CONSTRUCT CLASS WITH NO INTERFACE SPECIFICATION. Zero SUBSCRIBE or PUBLISH blocks, against 64 of 64, 71 of 71, 44 of 44 and 26 of 26 everywhere else. The framework even names an "Interface Exception Catalogue" that exists as no document.

• AND THOSE TWO GAPS COMPOUND. Four of the seven channels terminate in a layer that has no catalogue - steroid stress, interoception, light, and transgenerational epigenetics. These are exactly the routes a whole-organism architecture needs.

• THE GUIDING BOOK CARRIES NO TIME CONSTANTS. Zero occurrences, five uses of "milliseconds" in 10,935 lines - against 64, 71, 44 and 26 elsewhere. The one layer your ten architecture components are sourced from supplies no timescales. That is where OBSERVATION-23, opened today, actually comes from.

• THE RANKING IS UPSIDE DOWN. The north star and the guiding book, the two documents your rules rank highest, are the two least machine-readable in the library. The north star is 344,000 words of prose with no interface blocks, no code samples, no time constants and no modes companion.


THE CONTENT GAPS

• NO DEVELOPMENT AND NO DISEASE FIELD ON THE TWO LARGEST CATALOGUES. Layers 7, 8, 10 and 11 carry DISEASES AND DISORDERS on every entry. Layer 6 and Layer 9 carry it on none. That is 386 cell types and 123 regions with no failure semantics.

• LAYER 9'S PERIPHERAL HALF IS A STUB. Cortex 1,204 lines; spinal cord 32; enteric nervous system 16, for five hundred million neurons. No cranial nerve roster at all. Your Layer 10 systems are chains of Layer 9 places, and several links have no place to chain to.

• FOUR SYSTEMS NEVER ASSEMBLED. No cerebrovascular, clearance, neuroimmune or nociceptive system among the 44 - though every part exists at Layers 6 and 8. Pain is the sharpest: the somatosensory entry says pain's affective branch is "given its own address", and no such address exists.

• THE ACCEPTANCE SPECIFICATION OMITS THREE FACULTIES. Layer 12 names fourteen and returns zero hits for navigation, metacognition, imagination or prospection. A build could pass all fourteen suites while unable to navigate, unable to report what it does not know, and unable to simulate a future. The metacognition omission has safety consequences.

• NO ONTOGENY AXIS. "Ontogen" returns zero hits corpus-wide, as do "sexual differentiation" and "sexual dimorphism". The fifteen layers are a spatial ladder; Layer 15 is phylogeny. The architecture is specified as an adult steady state.


WHAT IT DOES UNUSUALLY WELL

The mode-register discipline is a genuine original contribution - no neuroscience reference specifies hybrid automata with admission rules, transfer functions, transition tables and fault signatures. Layer 9 describes contracts where textbooks describe tracts. And the corpus is current to 2026, ahead of every printed reference I checked.


ONE CAUTION

The corpus is governed as source. None of this should be written into those files as a side effect of a slice - each item is a candidate for the additive update run, or a question for the author. The line is whether the corpus is being corrected to match what it already delivers, or extended with new claims about the biology. Only the first is admissible without the author.

[ASSISTANT - 2026-08-12 08:44:41]

Full plan at [konnectome_neuroscience_library_fix_plan.txt](/home/ccaitwo/output/konnectome_neuroscience_library_fix_plan.txt). The short form:


THE CONSTRAINT THAT SHAPES EVERYTHING

Most of these gaps cannot be fixed by editing the corpus, and that is by design. There are exactly two routes in: a CORRECTNESS REPAIR, making a source file agree with what the corpus already delivers, and the CORPUS UPDATE RUN, which is additive only and may fold in only evidence ALREADY STANDING before the run began.

The run fires every tenth hand-off. The count is 42. The next run is at 50.

So anything meant to land in the corpus at hand-off fifty must be written as an evidence record before then. The evidence records are the long-lead item and they are cheap. They go first even though they land last.

A third route does not exist. Revising what the corpus claims about the biology is authorship, and it belongs to the author.


FOUR TRACKS

• TRACK TWO, FREE AND TODAY. I found one while writing this: three consecutive hand-offs, including the live one, say "the thirteen faculty suites". Layer 12 says FOURTEEN, three times. That is a wrong count about the corpus sitting in the build's own live instruction - a repair by the Sixteenth Commandment's own test. Then re-check every count konnectome restates, because the last time this build trusted an audit's own figure it was short by one.

• TRACK ONE, THE REAL WORK, NEEDS NOBODY'S PERMISSION. The headline item is the TIMESCALE DERIVATION TABLE. Layer 9 states no time constants - but the timescales exist, filed one layer away: Layer 7 has them on all 64 junctions, Layer 8 on all 71 motifs, Layer 10 on all 44 systems. A region's timescale is DERIVABLE from the layers either side of it. That is the Twenty-First Commandment's second protocol, so it needs no invented number and no corpus edit. Deliverable is docs/design/12, every cell marked CITED, DERIVED or REFUSED ALOUD.

• TRACK THREE, EVIDENCE NOW. Three records over the next few sessions: the four missing Layer 10 systems, the transcriptomic cross-walk, and pain. Pain is the strongest of them, because the corpus's own somatosensory entry PROMISES an address for pain's affective branch and no such entry exists - a corpus statement contradicted by corpus delivery.

• TRACK FOUR, NOT MINE TO ANSWER. Where ontogeny lives is a framework decision. "Adult steady state only" is a perfectly respectable answer that costs nothing, but it has to be SAID rather than drifted into. Likewise the 509 development-and-disease fields, and whether the north star should carry the schema at all.


WHY THE TIMESCALE TABLE GOES FIRST

It is queue item one wearing a different name. OBSERVATION-23 asks which calibration of the tick konnectome keeps, and that cannot be settled honestly without first measuring what the corpus states about timescales anywhere. Doing it the other way round means choosing a clock and then looking for support.


THE THING MOST LIKELY TO GO WRONG

Presenting a derived timescale as the corpus's. Tracks One point one and one point two both produce values that look exactly like corpus values and are not - which is this build's own named worst failure, an invented value that has learned to cite. Every cell gets marked, and every derivation gets shown.

Second most likely: letting the evidence clock run out. Eight sessions sounds like plenty and is three records. If they slip, the corpus waits another ten hand-offs, and nothing about that failure is visible at the time it happens.


I can start now with the count repairs and the timescale table if you want it moving; the Track Four questions I would hand you as one document rather than act on.

Research complete. Below is the report. (Per your instruction I wrote no files.)

DEVELOPMENTAL TIMETABLE OF THE HUMAN NERVOUS SYSTEM - VERIFIED QUANTITATIVE REPORT

Compiled 12 August 2026. Every number below is attributed to a named source. Where I could not verify a widely repeated figure against a source I could actually read, I say so explicitly rather than supply a number.


DATING CONVENTIONS - READ THIS FIRST

There are two clocks, and confusing them is the single largest source of error in this literature.

• Post-conception (PC), also called embryonic age, fertilisation age, or post-ovulatory age. The clock starts at fertilisation. Carnegie stages (the standard embryological staging system, 23 stages covering the first 8 weeks) are always post-conception.

• Post-menstrual (PM), also called gestational age (GA), the clinical convention. The clock starts at the first day of the mother's last menstrual period, roughly two weeks before fertilisation.

• The conversion is: gestational age = post-conception age + approximately 2 weeks.

• Embryology sources (Carnegie staging, neurulation day counts) use post-conception days. Imaging, obstetric and neonatal sources (fetal magnetic resonance imaging, preterm studies) use gestational weeks. I flag the convention for each item below.


1. NEURULATION

• Neural plate formation and primary neurulation span roughly the beginning of the third week to the end of the fourth week post-conception, corresponding to Carnegie stages 8 to 13. Source: Integrative Analysis of Key Signalling Pathways in Neural Tube Defects, PubMed Central article PMC12405674, 2025. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12405674/

• Closure initiates at the level of the future cervical spinal cord at approximately day 22 post-conception, forming what is called Closure Site 1, and the closure wave then propagates both rostrally (toward the head) and caudally (toward the tail). Same source.

• Anterior neuropore (rostral neuropore - the opening at the head end) closes at approximately day 25 post-conception. Sources: Neuroanatomy, Neural Tube Development and Stages, StatPearls, National Center for Biotechnology Information Bookshelf NBK557414. https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK557414/

• Posterior neuropore (caudal neuropore) closes at approximately days 27 to 28 post-conception. Failure of this closure, or its rupture shortly afterwards, produces spina bifida. Same StatPearls source, plus Formation of the Neural Tube, in Gilbert, Developmental Biology, NCBI Bookshelf NBK10080. https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK10080/

• Range and disagreement. Textbook values cluster tightly: anterior day 24 to 26, posterior day 26 to 28 post-conception. In post-menstrual terms that is roughly gestational week 5.4 to 6.0. The apparent day-level disagreement between texts is almost entirely a convention artefact plus the fact that Carnegie staging bins embryos by morphology, not by measured days since fertilisation. Treat "day 25" and "day 27" as central estimates with a plus or minus 1 to 2 day uncertainty, not as precise measurements.

• Practical anchor. Because closure completes before day 28 post-conception - roughly gestational week 6 - it is finished before most women know they are pregnant. This is the reason periconceptional folic acid supplementation must begin before conception.


2. NEUROGENESIS

Cortical neurogenesis

• The peak production window for cortical projection neurons in humans spans roughly gestational weeks 10 to 20 (post-menstrual), with substantial production continuing from about gestational week 8 to gestational week 25. Lineage-tracing and single-cell work sampling gestational weeks 16 to 24 documents an ongoing switch in progenitor output from glutamatergic (excitatory) to gamma-aminobutyric-acid-ergic, abbreviated GABAergic (inhibitory), neuron production across that window. Source: Lineage-resolved atlas of the developing human cortex, Nature, 2025. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-09033-8

• Reelin-expressing cells appear in the neocortical anlage (the earliest cortical primordium) at Carnegie stage 16 and increase at stages 17 to 18, that is roughly 5 to 8 gestational weeks. Source: Meyer and colleagues, Embryonic and Early Fetal Development of the Human Neocortex, Journal of Neuroscience, volume 20, 2000. https://www.jneurosci.org/content/20/5/1858.full

• Total neuron number in the human brain peaks at approximately gestational week 28 (post-menstrual), after which programmed cell death and pruning reduce it. Source: Tau, G.Z. and Peterson, B.S., Normal Development of Brain Circuits, Neuropsychopharmacology, 2010, PubMed Central PMC3055433. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3055433/

• Caution. The classic teaching that cortical neurogenesis is entirely complete by mid-gestation is a simplification. Interneuron production and, in particular, cerebellar granule cell production continue later, and the exact human end-point is not established with the precision that rodent data allow.

Adult hippocampal neurogenesis - the genuinely contested question

I report this as a live scientific dispute, because it is one. Do not present any single side as settled.

• Sorrells and colleagues, Human hippocampal neurogenesis drops sharply in children to undetectable levels in adults, Nature, volume 555, 2018. Immunohistochemistry on the dentate gyrus of 59 human subjects. Found neurogenesis robust in embryonic and infant tissue, dropping steeply with age, and detected none in any subject older than 13 years. https://www.nature.com/articles/nature25975

• Boldrini and colleagues, Human Hippocampal Neurogenesis Persists throughout Aging, Cell Stem Cell, 2018. Published within weeks of Sorrells and reached the opposite conclusion: neurogenesis persists in adults and, on their measures, does not markedly decline with age.

• Moreno-Jimenez and colleagues, Nature Medicine, 2019. Reported abundant immature neurons in neurologically healthy adults across the lifespan, with a sharp drop in Alzheimer's disease. Their central methodological claim was that tissue fixation protocol - specifically prolonged fixation - destroys the doublecortin signal, and that this explains negative findings.

• Frisén laboratory, Identification of proliferating neural progenitors in the adult human hippocampus, Science, volume 389, issue 6755, pages 58 to 63, 3 July 2025. Single-nucleus ribonucleic acid sequencing from birth through adulthood, using paediatric tissue to learn the progenitor transcriptional signature and machine-learning classifiers plus Ki67 proliferation-marker antibodies to find those cells in adults. Found proliferating neural progenitors localised to the dentate gyrus in adults - but with very large inter-individual variation, some adults having many and others almost none. https://www.science.org/doi/10.1126/science.adu9575

• Most recent entry: Human hippocampal neurogenesis in adulthood, ageing and Alzheimer's disease, Nature, 2026, PubMed identifier 41741649. Multiomic single-cell sequencing of 355,997 nuclei across young adults with intact memory, cognitively normal aged adults, so-called SuperAgers (over 80 with episodic memory matching people decades younger), and preclinical and clinical Alzheimer's disease. Reports neural stem cells, neuroblasts and immature granule neurons present, with neurogenesis stalled in Alzheimer's disease and elevated in SuperAgers. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10169-4

• Honest state of the evidence as of 2026. The balance has shifted toward "a low-level, highly variable adult process exists", helped considerably by the 2025 Science and 2026 Nature transcriptomic studies, which do not depend on the contested doublecortin antibody staining that drove the 2018 to 2019 stalemate. But the dispute is not closed. A 2025 review - Simard, Matosin and Mechawar, Adult Hippocampal Neurogenesis in the Human Brain: Updates, Challenges, and Perspectives, The Neuroscientist, 2025 - notes that diverging conclusions persist and that post-mortem interval, fixation duration, antigen retrieval, tissue quality, marker panel choice and analysis thresholding differ systematically across both positive and negative studies. https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/abs/10.1177/10738584241252581

• What is not supported. Any specific quantitative rate for adult human hippocampal neuron addition - for example the frequently repeated "700 new neurons per day per hippocampus" from Spalding and colleagues, Cell, 2013, based on carbon-14 birth dating - should be flagged as a single-study estimate with wide confidence intervals that no subsequent independent method has reproduced. Do not present it as an established constant.


3. NEURONAL MIGRATION

• Radial migration - young neurons climbing outward along radial glial fibres from the ventricular zone to the cortical plate - peaks between gestational weeks 12 and 20 (post-menstrual) and is largely complete by gestational weeks 26 to 29. Source: Tau and Peterson, Normal Development of Brain Circuits, Neuropsychopharmacology, 2010, PMC3055433.

• Inside-out layering. Cortical layers are built from the deepest outward: earlier-born neurons occupy deep layers (VI, then V), later-born neurons migrate past them to occupy progressively more superficial layers (IV, III, II). Layer I, the marginal zone, is a special case populated early by Cajal-Retzius cells. This inside-out rule is Rakic's radial unit hypothesis, and it is the standard account in Kandel, Principles of Neural Science, 6th edition, 2021.

• Reelin. A large secreted glycoprotein released by Cajal-Retzius neurons in the marginal zone. It provides the stop-and-detach signal that lets each migrating cohort pass the previously arrived cohort and settle superficially to it. Loss of Reelin signalling inverts the layering. Human Reelin-positive Cajal-Retzius populations are documented from Carnegie stage 16 onward, that is from roughly 5 to 6 gestational weeks. Sources: Meyer and colleagues, Journal of Neuroscience, 2000, https://www.jneurosci.org/content/20/5/1858.full ; and Subpial Granular Layer and Transient Versus Persisting Cajal-Retzius Neurons of the Fetal Human Cortex, Cerebral Cortex, volume 28, 2018. https://academic.oup.com/cercor/article/28/6/2043/3798184

• Radial migration pathway volumes measured by diffusion imaging show a left-greater-than-right hemispheric asymmetry in specimens of 22 gestational weeks or younger, and no significant asymmetry beyond 22 gestational weeks - consistent with radial migration winding down through the third trimester. Source: Asymmetry of Radial and Symmetry of Tangential Neuronal Migration Pathways in Developing Human Fetal Brains, PubMed Central PMC4724714. https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC4724714/

• Disagreement to flag. "Migration completes by gestational week 24" is a common textbook shorthand. The imaging and histology literature supports a later and softer endpoint - largely complete by gestational weeks 26 to 29, with tangential interneuron migration and cerebellar granule cell migration continuing beyond that, in the cerebellum into the early postnatal months.


4. AXON GUIDANCE

Mechanism, then the honest statement about human timing.

• Growth cone. The motile, hand-shaped tip of a growing axon, described by Ramon y Cajal in 1890. It samples the environment with filopodia (finger-like protrusions) and lamellipodia (sheet-like veils), and steers by differential actin assembly toward attractants and away from repellents.

• The four canonical guidance cue families, all of which act bifunctionally - attractive or repulsive depending on receptor complement and cyclic-nucleotide state of the growth cone:
◦ Netrins, acting through DCC (Deleted in Colorectal Cancer) receptors for attraction and UNC5 receptors for repulsion. Classical midline attractant for commissural axons.
◦ Slits, acting through Robo (Roundabout) receptors. Classical midline repellent; the Robo3 switch is what stops commissural axons re-crossing.
◦ Semaphorins, acting through Plexin and Neuropilin receptor complexes. Predominantly repulsive; Semaphorin 3C acting through Neuropilin-1 orients axons crossing the corpus callosum.
◦ Ephrins, acting through Eph receptor tyrosine kinases, with bidirectional (forward and reverse) signalling. Central to topographic mapping, for example retinotectal gradients.
Sources for family membership and callosal roles: Genetic heterogeneity in corpus callosum agenesis, Frontiers in Genetics, 2022, https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/genetics/articles/10.3389/fgene.2022.958570/full ; Development and plasticity of the corpus callosum, Development, volume 147, 2020, https://journals.biologists.com/dev/article/147/18/dev189738/225808/ ; Developmental Upregulation of Ephrin-B1 Silences Sema3C/Neuropilin-1 Signaling during Post-crossing Navigation of Corpus Callosum Axons, Current Biology, 2018.

• Human timing anchors that are actually documented:
◦ Corpus callosum development begins at approximately gestational week 12 (post-menstrual), the structure is complete and readily identifiable by gestational weeks 14 to 15, and the splenium (its posterior bulb) becomes prominent by gestational weeks 18 to 19. Source: Frontiers in Genetics, 2022, as above.
◦ Corticospinal tract myelination is already in progress at the pyramidal decussation by gestational week 25. Source: Correlation between human nervous system development and acquisition of fetal skills, Early Human Development, ScienceDirect. Axonal outgrowth necessarily precedes this.
◦ Thalamocortical, callosal and cortico-cortical pathway convergence and the dissolution of the subplate - a transient waiting compartment where thalamic axons pause before invading the cortical plate - dominate gestational weeks 24 to term. Source: Kostovic and colleagues, Fundamentals of the Development of Connectivity in the Human Fetal Brain in Late Gestation: From 24 Weeks Gestational Age to Term, Journal of Neuropathology and Experimental Neurology, volume 80, 2021, pages 393 onward. https://academic.oup.com/jnen/article/80/5/393/6212242

• Explicit caveat. Precise expression windows for netrins, slits, semaphorins and ephrins in the human fetal brain are far less well characterised than in mouse, chick and zebrafish. Almost every stated "cue X acts at time Y" in textbooks is extrapolated from model organisms. I found no authoritative human expression timetable with week-level resolution for all four families, and I will not fabricate one.


5. SYNAPTOGENESIS

The primary human dataset is Huttenlocher's post-mortem electron microscopy work, which remains unreplaced after nearly fifty years.

• Peak synaptogenic rate begins around gestational week 34 (post-menstrual), during which approximately 40,000 new synapses are formed every second. Source: Tau and Peterson, Normal Development of Brain Circuits, Neuropsychopharmacology, 2010, PMC3055433.

• Primary visual cortex (Brodmann area 17). A burst of synapse formation between 3 and 4 months of age, with synaptic density peaking at 140 to 150 percent of adult levels between 4 and 12 months of age. Sources: Huttenlocher, P.R. and Dabholkar, A.S., Regional differences in synaptogenesis in human cerebral cortex, Journal of Comparative Neurology, volume 387, issue 2, pages 167 to 178, 20 October 1997, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9336221/ ; as summarised in Tau and Peterson 2010.

• Prefrontal cortex (middle frontal gyrus). Synaptogenesis begins at about the same time as in visual cortex but does not enter its peak period until about 8 months of age, continuing through the second year. Maximum synaptic density in middle frontal gyrus is not reached until after 15 months of age. Huttenlocher and Dabholkar 1997.

• Auditory cortex. Intermediate in timing; net synapse elimination has ended in auditory cortex by about age 12 years, whereas in prefrontal cortex elimination extends into mid-adolescence. Huttenlocher and Dabholkar 1997.

• Frontal cortex original series. Huttenlocher, P.R., Synaptic Density in Human Frontal Cortex - Developmental Changes and Effects of Aging, Brain Research, volume 163, pages 195 to 205, 1979. This is the source of the canonical frontal-cortex density-versus-age curve.

• What I will NOT state. Absolute peak synaptic density expressed as synapses per cubic millimetre, or as synapses per neuron, is frequently quoted in secondary sources and textbooks. I could not verify a specific absolute value against Huttenlocher's primary text in this research pass. Figures in the order of 10 to the 9th synapses per cubic millimetre circulate widely; I am deliberately not asserting a number. What is solidly supported is the relative statement: peak density is roughly 140 to 150 percent of adult density in visual cortex, and childhood dendritic spine density exceeds adult values by two- to threefold in prefrontal cortex.

• Methodological caveat that applies to the whole dataset. Huttenlocher's regional-difference conclusions rest on a small number of post-mortem specimens, unevenly distributed in age, with essentially no specimens in some critical intervals. The smooth curves reproduced in textbooks are interpolations through sparse points. The ordering of regional peaks - visual before auditory before prefrontal - is robust; the exact ages of the peaks are not tightly constrained.


6. PROGRAMMED CELL DEATH (APOPTOSIS)

• Magnitude. Approximately 50 percent of neurons produced during development subsequently die. This figure is stated both in the general vertebrate literature - see Programmed Cell Death in the Developing Nervous System, Neuron, https://www.cell.com/neuron/fulltext/S0896-6273(00)80191-9 - and for humans specifically by Tau and Peterson, Neuropsychopharmacology, 2010, who state that as many as half of the neurons produced during neurogenesis have died by the end of adolescence.

• Range. Across neuronal populations and species the loss ranges roughly 20 to 80 percent depending on the population studied; the "about half" figure is a central tendency, not a constant. Motor neuron pools and sensory ganglia, the best-quantified populations, sit near 50 percent.

• Timing in humans. Neuron number peaks at approximately gestational week 28 (post-menstrual), placing the bulk of developmental apoptosis in the third trimester and the first postnatal year, with attrition continuing at a much lower rate through childhood and adolescence. Source: Tau and Peterson, 2010.

• Neurotrophic factor hypothesis. Formulated from Levi-Montalcini and Hamburger's nerve growth factor work and codified by Oppenheim, Cell Death During Development of the Nervous System, Annual Review of Neuroscience, volume 14, 1991. The claim: neurons are overproduced relative to the size of their target field; targets secrete limiting quantities of survival factors - nerve growth factor (NGF), brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF), neurotrophin-3, neurotrophin-4/5, glial-cell-line-derived neurotrophic factor; neurons that capture enough factor survive, those that do not activate the intrinsic apoptotic programme (Bax, cytochrome c release, Apaf-1, caspase-9, caspase-3). Its function is to quantitatively match neuron number to target size. See also Oppenheim, The neurotrophic factor concept: a reexamination, 1993, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/8331370/ , where he himself qualifies the strict target-competition model.

• Honest qualification. The neurotrophic hypothesis is best supported in the peripheral nervous system, where target-competition experiments are tractable. Its extension to the central nervous system - and particularly to cortex - is an extrapolation. Central neurons also die before ever contacting a target, via mechanisms including afferent deprivation and activity-independent early death. Do not present the hypothesis as a proven universal law of cortical development.


7. MYELINATION

The longest schedule of any developmental process in the nervous system: it begins in the second trimester and, in association cortex, is not finished until the fourth decade.

Fetal and infant histology

• Mature myelin appears first in subcortical regions and later in cortical regions across gestational weeks 20 to 28 (post-menstrual). Source: Tau and Peterson, 2010, PMC3055433.

• Corticospinal tract myelination is in progress at the pyramidal decussation by gestational week 25.

• Myelin detected in precentral and postcentral gyri (primary motor and primary somatosensory cortex) by gestational week 35.

• Myelin present in the acoustic radiation by gestational week 40.

• Between gestational weeks 36 and 40 the proportion of total brain volume occupied by myelinated white matter rises from 1 percent to 5 percent. Source: Tau and Peterson, 2010.

• Visual system myelination begins at about the 40th week in utero and is nearly complete a few months after birth. Source: Yakovlev and Lecours, as summarised in the Lancaster Glossary of Child Development, Myelination entry. https://www.lancaster.ac.uk/fas/psych/glossary/myelination/

Yakovlev and Lecours - the foundational reference

• Yakovlev, P.I. and Lecours, A.R., The myelogenetic cycles of regional maturation of the brain, in Minkowski, A., editor, Regional Development of the Brain in Early Life, Oxford, Blackwell, 1967, pages 3 to 70.

• Their central and still-standing finding: myelination does not proceed simultaneously across the brain but in a succession of regional waves. Order, earliest to latest: spinal roots and brainstem tracts, then sensory and motor projection systems (visual, auditory, somatosensory, corticospinal), then commissural and short association fibres, and last the intracortical fibres of association cortex - notably prefrontal and parietal association areas.

• Their most-cited quantitative claim: intracortical association-cortex fibres retain an immature greyish pallor beyond the third decade of life, that is beyond age 30. This is the histological origin of the modern "the prefrontal cortex myelinates last" statement.

• Caveat on the source. This is a 1967 book chapter based on stained post-mortem sections from an autopsy series, not a modern quantitative study. The regional ordering has been repeatedly confirmed; the precise terminal ages are eyeball estimates from stain intensity and should be treated as approximate.

Modern imaging corroboration

• Diffusion tensor imaging and magnetisation-transfer studies consistently reproduce the Yakovlev and Lecours ordering: fractional anisotropy - a diffusion imaging index that rises as axons become more coherently bundled and myelinated - matures earliest in projection tracts, later in commissural tracts, and latest in frontal and temporal association fibres, including the superior longitudinal fasciculus and the uncinate fasciculus.

• Bethlehem, R.A.I. and colleagues, Brain charts for the human lifespan, Nature, volume 604, April 2022, aggregating over 120,000 magnetic resonance imaging scans across the lifespan. Total white matter volume rises rapidly from mid-gestation through childhood and peaks just before age 29 years - the reported peak is 28.7 years. Cortical thickness peaks strikingly early, at 1.7 years. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-022-04554-y

• Grydeland and colleagues, Waves of Maturation and Senescence in Micro-structural MRI Markers of Human Cortical Myelination over the Lifespan, Cerebral Cortex, PubMed Central PMC6373687, which maps intracortical myelin-sensitive signal across the lifespan and finds region-specific maturational waves consistent with the histological ordering. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6373687/

• Mousley, A., Bethlehem, R.A.I., Yeh, F-C. and colleagues, Topological turning points across the human lifespan, Nature Communications, volume 16, article 10055, 2025, DOI 10.1038/s41467-025-65974-8. Diffusion imaging in 4,216 individuals aged 0 to 90, twelve graph-theory metrics of network organisation, projected via Uniform Manifold Approximation and Projection. Identifies four topological turning points at approximately ages 9, 32, 66 and 83 years, defining five epochs: childhood, adolescence, adulthood, early ageing, late ageing. Critically, the adolescence-to-adulthood transition falls at approximately age 32, not age 25. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-025-65974-8


8. ADOLESCENT SYNAPTIC PRUNING AND THE "AGE 25" CLAIM

Pruning - histology

• Petanjek, Z., Judas, M., Simic, G., Rasin, M.R., Uylings, H.B.M., Rakic, P. and Kostovic, I., Extraordinary neoteny of synaptic spines in the human prefrontal cortex, Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences USA, volume 108, number 32, pages 13281 to 13286, 9 August 2011. https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.1105108108

• Their sample: layer IIIC cortico-cortical and layer V cortico-subcortical projecting pyramidal neurons, Golgi-impregnated, in human prefrontal cortex from newborn to 91 years.

• Their headline findings: dendritic spine density in childhood exceeds adult values by two- to threefold. Spine density begins to fall during puberty. The decline continues well past adolescence, with adult-level spine density reached only in the third decade - approximately age 30. This late endpoint is the paper's "neoteny" claim: humans prune prefrontal spines for far longer than any other primate.

• Regional ordering of net synapse elimination, from Huttenlocher and Dabholkar 1997: elimination has ended in auditory cortex by about age 12 years; in prefrontal cortex it extends into mid-adolescence and, on Petanjek's data, beyond.

• Magnitude, stated honestly. A two- to threefold childhood excess implies a loss on the order of 50 to 65 percent of peak spine density in prefrontal cortex over the pruning period. The frequently quoted round figure "about 40 percent of synapses are lost in adolescence" traces to Huttenlocher's frontal cortex curve. Both are defensible orders of magnitude; neither is a precisely measured constant, and the underlying specimen counts are small. I have not asserted a single authoritative percentage because the primary sources do not supply one with confidence intervals.

Grey matter volume trajectories - imaging

• Bethlehem and colleagues, Nature, 2022. Total grey matter volume peaks at 5.9 years. Regional grey matter volume peaks vary widely across the 34 cortical areas measured, from approximately 2 years to approximately 10 years. Cortical thickness peaks at 1.7 years. White matter volume peaks at 28.7 years.

• The important interpretive point: the adolescent decline in cortical grey matter measured by magnetic resonance imaging is NOT a direct measurement of synapse loss. It is a composite signal, substantially driven by increasing intracortical myelination shifting the grey-white boundary inward, and by changes in tissue water content, in addition to any true synaptic and dendritic remodelling. The routine equation of "grey matter thinning" with "pruning" in popular accounts is an overreach.

The "age 25" claim - assessment: OVERSTATED

• Verdict: the claim that the prefrontal cortex "finishes maturing at 25" is not well supported as stated. It is a hardened rounding of a soft and heterogeneous finding.

• Origin of the number. Early structural magnetic resonance imaging cohorts of adolescent brain development largely stopped recruiting around age 20 to 25. Because grey matter trajectories were still changing at the upper edge of the sampled range, investigators could only say maturation continued past the data. "Mid-twenties" became a hedge, and the hedge hardened into "25". No study identified 25 as an inflection point. Documented in the Wikipedia entry Brain matures at 25 myth, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain_matures_at_25_myth , and in Slate, Brain development: The myth the brain "matures" when you're 25, November 2022, https://slate.com/technology/2022/11/brain-development-25-year-old-mature-myth.html

• Individual variation defeats the population claim. Somerville, L.H., Searching for Signatures of Brain Maturity: What Are We Searching For?, Neuron, volume 92, 2016 - some 8-year-old brains show a higher composite "maturation index" than some 25-year-old brains. A population-mean age therefore cannot classify an individual.

• Best current evidence points later than 25, not at it. Petanjek 2011 puts prefrontal spine pruning endpoint near age 30. Bethlehem 2022 puts white matter volume peak at 28.7 years. Mousley and colleagues 2025 put the adolescent-to-adult network topology turning point at approximately age 32. Three independent methods - Golgi histology, volumetric magnetic resonance imaging, and diffusion network topology - converge on late twenties to early thirties, not 25.

• There is also no single maturational endpoint. Different measures - cortical thickness, grey matter volume, white matter volume, myelin content, network topology, functional connectivity - peak at different ages spanning roughly 1.7 years to 32 years. Asking "when does the brain finish developing" presupposes a single answer that the data do not contain.

• Where the claim retains force. The directional statement - that prefrontal, executive-control and inhibitory systems mature later than limbic and reward systems, producing a temporal gap during adolescence - is well supported and is the substantive finding underlying the myth. The dual-systems account (Steinberg, Casey) rests on this and survives. What does not survive is the specific number 25, its use as a bright line, and its application to individuals, including in legal and policy settings.


SUMMARY TIMELINE

• Days 22 to 28 post-conception (gestational weeks 5 to 6): neural tube closes; anterior neuropore approximately day 25, posterior approximately day 27.

• Gestational weeks 8 to 25 (post-menstrual), peaking weeks 10 to 20: cortical neurogenesis.

• Gestational weeks 12 to 20, largely complete by weeks 26 to 29: radial neuronal migration, inside-out layering, Reelin-dependent.

• Gestational weeks 12 to term: axon guidance, corpus callosum from week 12, recognisable by weeks 14 to 15; subplate-mediated thalamocortical ingrowth weeks 24 to term.

• Gestational week 20 onward: myelination begins, subcortical first.

• Gestational week 28: total neuron number peaks; apoptosis then removes approximately half.

• Gestational week 34 onward: peak synaptogenesis, roughly 40,000 new synapses per second.

• 4 to 12 months postnatal: visual cortex synaptic density peaks at 140 to 150 percent of adult.

• After 15 months postnatal: prefrontal cortex synaptic density peaks.

• 1.7 years: cortical thickness peaks.

• 5.9 years: total grey matter volume peaks.

• Age 12: net synapse elimination complete in auditory cortex.

• Puberty to approximately age 30: prefrontal dendritic spine pruning, two- to threefold reduction.

• 28.7 years: white matter volume peaks.

• Approximately 32 years: structural network topology transition from adolescent to adult regime.

• Beyond the third decade: association-cortex intracortical myelination completes.


FLAGGED AS WEAKLY SUPPORTED OR UNVERIFIED

• Absolute synapses per cubic millimetre at peak - not verified against the primary Huttenlocher text; I supplied no number.

• "700 new hippocampal neurons per day" (Spalding 2013) - single-study, single-method, unreproduced.

• "The brain matures at 25" - not supported by any study; an artefact of cohort upper age limits.

• Yakovlev and Lecours terminal myelination ages - qualitative stain-intensity estimates from 1967, not quantitative measurements.

• Precise human expression windows for netrin, slit, semaphorin and ephrin families - extrapolated from model organisms; no human week-level timetable located.

• "Migration complete by gestational week 24" - textbook shorthand; primary sources support weeks 26 to 29 with later tangential and cerebellar migration.</result>
