<summary>Agent "Research vagal + neuroimmune" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>ive signal traces to a small number of single-centre trials, prominently Akhondzadeh et al 2009 (Depression and Anxiety), whose data integrity and reporting have been questioned. Earlier positive pooled estimates such as Na et al 2014 should be discounted accordingly.

• Mac Giollabhui N and colleagues 2025/2026, American Journal of Psychiatry, doi 10.1176/appi.ajp.20241115 (PMC12910469). Preregistered meta-analysis restricted to depressed people with elevated inflammation. In the CRP at or above 2 mg/L stratum: depressive symptom severity Hedges g = 0.35 (0.05 to 0.64, p = 0.022, k = 11, n = 321); anhedonia g = 0.40 (0.08 to 0.71, p = 0.013, k = 4, n = 163). With CRP at or above 3 mg/L, g = 0.28 (0.01 to 0.55, p = 0.04). Crucially, response (risk ratio 1.28, 0.997 to 1.64, p = 0.053; 49 versus 41 percent) and remission (risk ratio 1.18, 0.71 to 1.95; 23 versus 21 percent) were NOT significant. Serious adverse events risk ratio 1.08.

• That split result explains the divergent coverage: some outlets headlined promise, others (Mad in America, December 2025) headlined failure. Both readings are defensible - a small continuous-scale effect with no proven categorical clinical benefit.

• Pooled estimates disagree sharply across analyses. A 2024 Frontiers in Psychiatry meta-analysis of 48 studies reported an odds ratio of 2.04 (1.41 to 2.97) for anti-inflammatory versus placebo, with non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs ranking best on acceptability but efficacy comparable to placebo. That much larger effect almost certainly reflects inclusion of low-quality and unstratified trials. The American Journal of Psychiatry figure is the better-controlled estimate.


B9. MENDELIAN RANDOMISATION AND THE CRP-VERSUS-IL-6 DISSOCIATION

• Mendelian randomisation uses inherited genetic variants as natural randomisers to test causation.

• Direction of evidence: interleukin-6 signalling, not CRP, looks causal. Kappelmann N, Arloth J, Georgakis MK, Czamara D, Rost N, Ligthart S, Khandaker GM, Binder EB 2021, JAMA Psychiatry, found genetically upregulated IL-6 signalling associated with suicidality and specific depressive symptoms, robust across instrument-selection strategies.

• The dissociation. When CRP is instrumented using IL6R variants, higher CRP predicts LOWER odds of depression - because those variants block classical IL-6 signalling, raising circulating CRP while reducing downstream signalling. Instruments acting on CRP through other pathways do not reproduce this. CRP is therefore a downstream readout and a poor causal target, while genetically higher IL-6 activity together with genetically lower CRP maps onto higher depressive-symptom risk (Kappelmann et al 2020; Khandaker group).

• Kelly KM and colleagues 2021, Brain Behavior and Immunity (UK Biobank), "Depression and interleukin-6 signaling": results consistent with a causal effect of soluble IL-6 receptor on depression, compatible with either reduced classical or increased trans-signalling.

• Counterweight, and the field is not settled. The HUNT study Mendelian randomisation (Psychological Medicine 2023/2024, PMC10755231) found little support for a causal role of CRP or IL-6 on anxiety-depression symptoms or life satisfaction. The Lifelines cohort (Translational Psychiatry 2025, s41398-025-03372-w) similarly reports genetic evidence weaker than observational. Bidirectional Mendelian randomisation also raises reverse causation, since depression and its correlates (adiposity, smoking) raise CRP (Scientific Reports 2022, s41598-022-19214-4).

• What would settle it: well-powered cis-instrument Mendelian randomisation on IL6R, IL6 and gp130 in newer and larger depression genome-wide association studies with symptom-level phenotypes, plus randomised IL-6 blockade at adequate scale.


B10. 2024-2026 STATUS OF IMMUNOPSYCHIATRY

• Stratification by CRP is now the dominant design assumption. INSIGHT (Khandaker, Bristol) randomised single-dose tocilizumab, an IL-6 receptor blocker, in depression with high-sensitivity CRP at or above 3 mg/L.

• The follow-on proof-of-concept trial, "Interleukin 6 as a treatment target for depression", JAMA Psychiatry, 20 May 2026, randomised 30 patients with difficult-to-treat depression (14 tocilizumab, 16 placebo) over 4 weeks. Remission was 54 versus 31 percent, number needed to treat about 5 (against roughly 7 for selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors), with improvements in fatigue, anxiety and quality of life - but there was NO statistically significant between-group difference, as expected at n = 30. Hypothesis-generating only. https://www.bristol.ac.uk/news/2026/may/pilot-trial-suggests-anti.html

• Ongoing: INSTA-MD (protocol, PMC11440344), inflammation-based stratification for add-on celecoxib or minocycline in major depressive disorder, double-blind placebo-controlled. A Brazilian triple-blind add-on tocilizumab trial in treatment-resistant depression with low-grade inflammation is registered (NCT07052058).

• The closest thing to a consensus position is the American Journal of Psychiatry 2025 editorial "Advancing an Inflammatory Subtype of Major Depression" (doi 10.1176/appi.ajp.20250289): an inflammatory endotype is plausible and worth prospective enrichment, but no formal consensus statement establishes a validated CRP threshold, and no anti-inflammatory agent is licensed for depression anywhere.

• Bottom line for Topic B4. Prevalence figures are solid. Cross-sectional effect sizes are solid but confounded by adiposity, smoking and illness. Causal genetics points to IL-6 signalling rather than CRP. Clinical benefit in CRP-enriched samples is small (Hedges g approximately 0.28 to 0.40) with no proven effect on response or remission. Adequately powered, prospectively CRP-enriched trials of IL-6 blockade are what would settle it.


PRINCIPAL UNRESOLVED QUESTIONS ACROSS BOTH TOPICS

• Human neuropod synapses have never been demonstrated. Almost all neuropod physiology is mouse; the main human bridge, Beumer 2024 Science, validates human enteroendocrine receptor-to-hormone sensing but not synaptic connectivity. Volume electron microscopy of human intestinal biopsies would settle it.

• What fraction of enteroendocrine cells are actually synaptically connected neuropod cells has never been quantified for any species.

• No direction-assigned human vagal fibre count exists, so the 80 percent afferent figure remains a cat extrapolation.

• The relative quantitative contribution of the five blood-to-brain routes - barrier transport, circumventricular organs, vagal afferents, endothelial prostaglandin E2, and skull-marrow and meningeal immunity - has never been apportioned. Each has been shown necessary in some paradigm and dispensable in another. Route-specific, dose-matched, simultaneous blockade experiments would be needed.

• Whether perivascular macrophage or endothelial COX-2 supplies the substrate for early-phase fever.

• Whether the human skull-marrow-to-meninges route carries functionally significant immune traffic or whether the TSPO-PET skull signal is an epiphenomenon.

• The true healthy human plasma concentration of IL-1beta, which sits below the reliable detection floor of most assays.

• The absolute number of microglia in the human brain, derived from percentage estimates that themselves span 0.5 to 16.6 percent.

• Whether the RECOVER trial for vagus nerve stimulation in depression should be read as a failure (missed pre-registered primary endpoint) or a success (concordant secondaries). Both readings are in print and no second adequately powered trial exists.


THREE PREMISE CORRECTIONS WORTH CARRYING FORWARD

• Buchanan et al 2022 is Nature Neuroscience, not Nature.

• There is no Eisenberger 2010 Biological Psychiatry paper on social disconnection. The Biological Psychiatry 2010 paper concerns anhedonia and ventral striatum; the social-disconnection paper is Brain, Behavior, and Immunity 2010; the dorsal anterior cingulate and sex findings are NeuroImage 2009. All three report the same 39-participant trial, so their combined citation count overstates the independent evidence.

• The neuropod "milliseconds" figure is 60 to 800 milliseconds for synaptic latency in co-culture. Whole-nerve vagal responses in the intact animal peak at 70 to 95 seconds. Both numbers come from the same paper, and the second is routinely omitted.</result>

<summary>Agent "Research neurotransmitter chemistry" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>ation was tested and largely rejected by the PLoS ONE 2011 neuropil study.

• Intravesicular acetylcholine: approximately 100 to 300 mM (textbook band) versus approximately 500 to 600 mM (Torpedo electric organ preparations, described as "near the molar range"). Two- to five-fold gap. Likely causes: Torpedo vesicles are larger than mammalian central vesicles, isolation losses, and the free-versus-gel-bound ambiguity.

• Intravesicular glutamate: quoted band 60 to 250 mM, but 8,000 molecules in a roughly 30 nm-diameter lumen implies of order 900 mM by naive geometry. Unreconciled. The gel-matrix hypothesis (PNAS 2003) is the standard escape, but it means "concentration" inside a vesicle may not be a well-defined thermodynamic quantity at all.

• Cleft peak glutamate: approximately 1.1 mM with a 1.2 ms decay time constant (Clements et al., Science 1992, NMDA-receptor antagonist-displacement kinetics) versus approximately 3 mM (Diamond and Jahr 1997, AMPA-receptor kinetics plus diffusion modelling). Both are model-dependent inferences; neither is a direct measurement, and a direct measurement in a 20 nm cleft is probably not achievable.

• Glutamate molecules per vesicle: 4,000 at the low end of common citation versus 7,000 (calyx of Held receptor modelling, J Physiol 2013) and approximately 8,000 (direct amperometry, JACS 2019). The two best modern methods converge at 7,000 to 8,000; the number is genuinely variable across vesicles, not fixed.

• Acetylcholine molecules per quantum: Kuffler and Yoshikami 1975 concluded "LESS THAN 10,000" - an upper bound, routinely miscited as a point estimate of 10,000.

• Tyrosine hydroxylase Km for tyrosine: 5.1 plus/minus 2.2 micromolar (Fossbakk et al.) versus approximately 50 micromolar (ScienceDirect overview). Roughly tenfold.

• Tyrosine hydroxylase Km / S0.5 for BH4: 39 plus/minus 8.4 micromolar (Fossbakk) versus approximately 5 micromolar (overview) versus 24 plus/minus 4 micromolar falling to 11 plus/minus 2 after Ser40 phosphorylation (Flatmark 1999). Also disputed whether it is a Km at all, given negative cooperativity.

• TPH Km for tryptophan: 16.6 to 19.2 micromolar at 400 micromolar BH4 versus 41 micromolar for wild-type TPH2. Roughly 15 to 50 micromolar band.

• Acetylcholinesterase kcat: 5,000; 10,000; 14,000; 15,000; 16,000; 25,000 per second across retrieved sources. Order of magnitude 10 to the 4th is SECURE; the precise value is not. Species, temperature, ionic strength, and per-molecule versus per-active-site (electric eel enzyme has 2 sites per molecule) explain most of the spread.

• AChE catalytic efficiency: 1.5 x 10 to the 8th versus 2 x 10 to the 8th per molar per second - both internally consistent with the quoted kcat and Km.

• "Fastest known enzyme" claim for AChE: CONTRADICTED. Catalase approximately 4 x 10 to the 7th per second and carbonic anhydrase approximately 10 to the 6th per second have far higher kcat; superoxide dismutase has higher kcat/Km at approximately 2 x 10 to the 9th per molar per second.

• COMT Val158Met effect: three-to-fourfold (Lachman 1996, in vitro) versus approximately 40 percent (Chen 2004, postmortem human brain). Not reconcilable as one number.

• MAOA cytogenetic band: Xp11.3 versus Xp11.23 versus Xp11.4-p11.23.

• MAO-B age-related increase: direction well supported, the widely repeated "fourfold" magnitude NOT verifiable against a primary source.

• GLT-1 turnover: approximately 15 per second (commonly quoted) versus 19 per second measured at 37 degrees Celsius and minus 50 millivolts.

• Vigabatrin visual toxicity mechanism: optic-nerve myelin oedema versus rod and cone photoreceptor signalling. The retinal account has stronger backing.

NUMBERS THAT COULD NOT BE ESTABLISHED AT ALL THIS SESSION - do not invent these

• GAD65 and GAD67 Km for L-glutamate (BRENDA entry for EC 4.1.1.15 exposed no value).
• Tyrosine hydroxylase Km for O2.
• TPH1 substrate-inhibition Ki (ACS full text paywalled).
• Histidine decarboxylase Km for L-histidine.
• MAO-A and MAO-B Km for dopamine and 2-phenylethylamine with human recombinant enzyme - published MAO kinetics overwhelmingly use surrogate substrates (kynuramine, fluorogenic probes) because the natural amines are hard to assay. Do not quote a "textbook" dopamine Km.
• A pooled effect size for tyrosine supplementation - Jongkees et al. 2015 is a narrative review with no meta-analytic estimate.


SECTION 12 - FIVE FRAMING POINTS THE CHAPTER SHOULD MAKE

• The six-order-of-magnitude cascade. Roughly 100 mM or more inside the vesicle, 1 to 3 mM peak in the cleft, roughly 25 nanomolar ambient - across a 20 nm gap in under a millisecond. Every enzyme, transporter and cofactor in this chapter exists to maintain that gradient.

• Most brain glutamate is not a neurotransmitter. The 10 to 12 micromol per gram tissue figure is dominated by the metabolic pool - a tricarboxylic acid cycle intermediate, nitrogen carrier, and precursor of GABA and glutathione. Magnetic resonance spectroscopy studies reporting patient "glutamate levels" are measuring that pool, not the signalling pool.

• Degradation is not termination. For monoamines and glutamate, transporters end the signal; MAO and COMT work on transmitter already retrieved. Acetylcholine is the sole classical exception, and it pays for that exception with an enzyme running at the diffusion limit. The trade-off is visible in the numbers: AChE at approximately 10 to the 4th per second versus DAT and NET at approximately 1.3 per second. Transporters compensate with copy number - GLT-1 is approximately 1 percent of total brain protein.

• The synapse is a three-cell structure. Glutamine synthetase is astrocyte-exclusive, so glutamatergic neurons cannot make their own transmitter precursor. The carbon-13 magnetic resonance spectroscopy flux numbers (Vcyc 0.26 plus/minus 0.08 micromol per gram per minute in humans; a slope of 0.89 plus/minus 0.06 between neuronal tricarboxylic acid cycling and glutamate-glutamine cycling) say that the brain's energy budget IS, to first approximation, the cost of glutamatergic signalling.

• The blood-brain barrier is a regulator, not a funnel. It actively pumps glutamate OUT. Precursor loading works only where a rate-limiting enzyme is genuinely unsaturated (tryptophan hydroxylase) or where the system was artificially depleted first. Tyrosine hydroxylase has three independent brakes - near-saturation, catecholamine feedback with a dopamine IC50 of 0.49 micromolar against roughly 2 micromolar free cytosolic dopamine, and substrate inhibition above about 59 micromolar tyrosine. That is why tyrosine loading does nothing in a rested person, and it is chemistry, not opinion.
